I might’ve gotten a little carried away with it… I kept moving the controls, kind of jerking them around to demonstrate, and people were yelling for me to stop.\n\nI’m not proud of it. I just… I couldn’t seem to make myself stop right then, even though I knew it probably looked bad. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has anything like this ever happened before, where you felt unable to stop a behavior even though you knew it might be inappropriate?\n\nPatient: I mean… the main thing is that fair incident. I was up there giving my talk, and I climbed onto the tractor to show the crowd what I meant by that “new pattern.” I kept jerking the controls around, trying to demonstrate it, and even when people were yelling at me to stop, I just… couldn’t seem to make myself quit right away.\n\nI know that doesn’t sound good. I wasn’t trying to cause trouble. It just got away from me, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Before you started jerking the tractor controls at the fair, did you feel a strong, sudden urge or need to do it — like you had to act on it even if you weren’t planning to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a sudden urge exactly, Doctor… I wasn’t planning on making a scene or anything. I was just trying to show the crowd that “new pattern,” and once I climbed up on the tractor and started moving the controls, I kind of kept going with it.\n\nEven when folks were yelling at me to stop, I… I couldn’t seem to make myself stop right away. I know that sounds bad. I really was trying to demonstrate something, not scare anybody.\n\nDoctor: Before you started moving the tractor controls, did you feel like the urge to do it was coming from outside of you or that someone or something else was putting the thought into your head?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not like somebody put it in my head, no. At least, that’s not how I remember it.\n\nIt felt more like I got focused on showing what I meant, and then I was already doing it before I really thought it through. I know that probably still wasn’t right, but I wouldn’t say it came from outside me. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: While you were moving the tractor controls at the fair, did you feel like you were in a daze or not fully aware of what was happening around you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I was in a daze exactly… I knew I was at the fair, and I knew people were there watching. I just got so caught up in showing that “new pattern” on the tractor that I kept jerking the controls, even after folks started yelling at me to stop.\n\nI’m not proud of that. It wasn’t like I meant to ignore them, but in the moment I couldn’t seem to make myself stop right away. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: While you were moving the tractor controls, did you feel like you were acting automatically, as if you were on autopilot and not fully in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yeah… not like I was unconscious or anything. I knew I was up there, and I knew people were watching.\n\nBut once I started jerking the controls to show that “new pattern,” it sort of got away from me. It just felt like I wasn’t fully steering myself in that moment, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when you felt like you were acting automatically or not fully aware of what you were doing, outside of that day at the fair?\n\nPatient: I can’t think of a lot of other times like that, no… not the same way.\n\nThe fair was the main one they brought me in for. It just got away from me, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Before you started jerking the tractor controls at the fair, did you feel a strong, sudden urge or need to do it — like you had to act on it even if you weren’t planning to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a sudden urge exactly, Doctor… I wasn’t planning on making a scene or anything. I was just trying to show the crowd that “new pattern,” and once I climbed up on the tractor and started moving the controls, I kind of kept going with it.\n\nEven when folks were yelling at me to stop, I… I couldn’t seem to make myself stop right away. I know that sounds bad. I really was trying to demonstrate something, not scare anybody.\n\nDoctor: Before you started moving the tractor controls, did you feel like the urge to do it was coming from outside of you or that someone or something else was putting the thought into your head?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not like somebody put it in my head, no. At least, that’s not how I remember it.\n\nIt felt more like I got focused on showing what I meant, and then I was already doing it before I really thought it through. I know that probably still wasn’t right, but I wouldn’t say it came from outside me. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: While you were moving the tractor controls at the fair, did you feel like you were in a daze or not fully aware of what was happening around you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I was in a daze exactly… I knew I was at the fair, and I knew people were there watching. I just got so caught up in showing that “new pattern” on the tractor that I kept jerking the controls, even after folks started yelling at me to stop.\n\nI’m not proud of that. It wasn’t like I meant to ignore them, but in the moment I couldn’t seem to make myself stop right away. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: While you were moving the tractor controls, did you feel like you were acting automatically, as if you were on autopilot and not fully in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yeah… not like I was unconscious or anything. I knew I was up there, and I knew people were watching.\n\nBut once I started jerking the controls to show that “new pattern,” it sort of got away from me. People were yelling for me to stop, and I should’ve stopped right then, I know that… but I couldn’t seem to make myself quit right away. I don’t want to make it sound like I meant to cause trouble. It just felt like I wasn’t fully steering myself in that moment, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when you felt like you were acting automatically or not fully aware of what you were doing, outside of that day at the fair?\n\nPatient: I can’t think of a lot of other times like that, no… not the same way.\n\nThe fair was the main one they brought me in for. I climbed up on that tractor during my talk, and I got to jerking the controls to show everybody that “new pattern” I was trying to explain. People were yelling at me to stop, and I… I just didn’t stop right away, even though I should have.\n\nOutside of that, I don’t know that I’d say I’ve had another episode quite like it. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but that day, yeah, it did feel like I wasn’t fully in charge of myself for a bit. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: While you were moving the tractor controls at the fair, did you feel confused about who you were or feel like you were watching yourself from the outside?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it was like that. I knew I was me, and I don’t remember feeling like I was floating outside myself or watching it happen from somewhere else.\n\nI was embarrassed after, and I know I should’ve stopped sooner, but at the time it wasn’t confusion about who I was. More like I got wrapped up in what I was doing. Is that the distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself doing something automatically or without full awareness, even briefly, outside of the fair incident — like you were going through the motions without being fully in control?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, everybody has moments where you’re doing chores and your mind wanders a little, but nothing like that fair thing, where I felt like I couldn’t stop right away.\n\nI haven’t noticed myself blanking out or doing things without knowing it, if that’s what you mean. At least, not that I can point to. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any moments, even brief ones, where you felt like you were acting automatically or not fully aware of what you were doing, even outside of the fair incident?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know I keep circling back to the same thing… but the fair is really the only one that stands out.\n\nWhen they brought me in, it was because I’d climbed up onto that tractor during my talk, and I was jerking the controls around trying to show the crowd this “new pattern” I had in my head. People were yelling for me to stop, and I heard them, but I just… didn’t stop right away. It felt like I couldn’t quite make myself quit in that moment.\n\nOutside of that, I can’t honestly think of another time like that. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m worse than I am, but that one did happen. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a moment where you suddenly realized you were doing something but couldn’t remember why you started doing it in the first place?\n\nPatient: Not that I can really remember, no. I mean, I’ve walked into the barn and forgotten what I went in there for, that kind of normal thing, but I don’t think I’ve suddenly “come to” doing something and had no idea why I started.\n\nIf there’s something specific you mean, I can try to think harder, but I don’t want to make up an answer just to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any episodes where you were doing something and later realized you didn’t fully remember doing it, even briefly, such as finding yourself somewhere without knowing how you got there?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had that happen. Not like finding myself somewhere and not knowing how I got there.\n\nI can forget little things, like where I set a tool down or why I walked over to the shed, but I remember getting there. I haven’t noticed any gaps like that. I hope that’s the kind of thing you’re asking.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess they wanted me to come in after something that happened at the fair. I was giving a talk, and I got up on the tractor to show folks what I meant by a new pattern. I might’ve gotten a little carried away with it… I kept moving the controls, kind of jerking them around to demonstrate, and people were yelling for me to stop.\n\nI’m not proud of it. I just… I couldn’t seem to make myself stop right then, even though I knew it probably looked bad. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has anything like this ever happened before, where you felt unable to stop a behavior even though you knew it might be inappropriate?\n\nPatient: I mean… the main thing is that fair incident. I was up there giving my talk, and I climbed onto the tractor to show the crowd what I meant by that “new pattern.” I kept jerking the controls around, trying to demonstrate it, and even when people were yelling at me to stop, I just… couldn’t seem to make myself quit right away.\n\nI know that doesn’t sound good. I wasn’t trying to cause trouble. It just got away from me, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Before you started jerking the tractor controls at the fair, did you feel a strong, sudden urge or need to do it — like you had to act on it even if you weren’t planning to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a sudden urge exactly, Doctor… I wasn’t planning on making a scene or anything. I was just trying to show the crowd that “new pattern,” and once I climbed up on the tractor and started moving the controls, I kind of kept going with it.\n\nEven when folks were yelling at me to stop, I… I couldn’t seem to make myself stop right away. I know that sounds bad. I really was trying to demonstrate something, not scare anybody.\n\nDoctor: Before you started moving the tractor controls, did you feel like the urge to do it was coming from outside of you or that someone or something else was putting the thought into your head?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not like somebody put it in my head, no. At least, that’s not how I remember it.\n\nIt felt more like I got focused on showing what I meant, and then I was already doing it before I really thought it through. I know that probably still wasn’t right, but I wouldn’t say it came from outside me. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: While you were moving the tractor controls at the fair, did you feel like you were in a daze or not fully aware of what was happening around you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I was in a daze exactly… I knew I was at the fair, and I knew people were there watching. I just got so caught up in showing that “new pattern” on the tractor that I kept jerking the controls, even after folks started yelling at me to stop.\n\nI’m not proud of that. It wasn’t like I meant to ignore them, but in the moment I couldn’t seem to make myself stop right away. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: While you were moving the tractor controls, did you feel like you were acting automatically, as if you were on autopilot and not fully in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yeah… not like I was unconscious or anything. I knew I was up there, and I knew people were watching.\n\nBut once I started jerking the controls to show that “new pattern,” it sort of got away from me. People were yelling for me to stop, and I should’ve stopped right then, I know that… but I couldn’t seem to make myself quit right away. I don’t want to make it sound like I meant to cause trouble. It just felt like I wasn’t fully steering myself in that moment, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when you felt like you were acting automatically or not fully aware of what you were doing, outside of that day at the fair?\n\nPatient: I can’t think of a lot of other times like that, no… not the same way.\n\nThe fair was the main one they brought me in for. I climbed up on that tractor during my talk, and I got to jerking the controls to show everybody that “new pattern” I was trying to explain. People were yelling at me to stop, and I… I just didn’t stop right away, even though I should have.\n\nOutside of that, I don’t know that I’d say I’ve had another episode quite like it. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but that day, yeah, it did feel like I wasn’t fully in charge of myself for a bit. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: While you were moving the tractor controls at the fair, did you feel confused about who you were or feel like you were watching yourself from the outside?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it was like that. I knew I was me, and I don’t remember feeling like I was floating outside myself or watching it happen from somewhere else.\n\nI was embarrassed after, and I know I should’ve stopped sooner, but at the time it wasn’t confusion about who I was. More like I got wrapped up in what I was doing. Is that the distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself doing something automatically or without full awareness, even briefly, outside of the fair incident — like you were going through the motions without being fully in control?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, everybody has moments where you’re doing chores and your mind wanders a little, but nothing like that fair thing, where I felt like I couldn’t stop right away.\n\nI haven’t noticed myself blanking out or doing things without knowing it, if that’s what you mean. At least, not that I can point to. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any moments, even brief ones, where you felt like you were acting automatically or not fully aware of what you were doing, even outside of the fair incident?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know I keep circling back to the same thing… but the fair is really the only one that stands out.\n\nWhen they brought me in, it was because I’d climbed up onto that tractor during my talk, and I was jerking the controls around trying to show the crowd this “new pattern” I had in my head. People were yelling for me to stop, and I heard them, but I just… didn’t stop right away. It felt like I couldn’t quite make myself quit in that moment.\n\nOutside of that, I can’t honestly think of another time like that. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m worse than I am, but that one did happen. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a moment where you suddenly realized you were doing something but couldn’t remember why you started doing it in the first place?\n\nPatient: Not that I can really remember, no. I mean, I’ve walked into the barn and forgotten what I went in there for, that kind of normal thing, but I don’t think I’ve suddenly “come to” doing something and had no idea why I started.\n\nIf there’s something specific you mean, I can try to think harder, but I don’t want to make up an answer just to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any episodes where you were doing something and later realized you didn’t fully remember doing it, even briefly, such as finding yourself somewhere without knowing how you got there?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had that happen. Not like finding myself somewhere and not knowing how I got there.\n\nI can forget little things, like where I set a tool down or why I walked over to the shed, but I remember getting there. I haven’t noticed any gaps like that. I hope that’s the kind of thing you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any strange sensations, like a rising feeling in your stomach or a strange taste, right before doing something automatic or without full awareness?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t believe so. I haven’t noticed anything like a rising feeling in my stomach, or a funny taste, before something like that.\n\nI mean, I get nerves sometimes like anybody, but not some warning sign right before losing track or doing something automatic. If it happened, I don’t remember it standing out. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever, even briefly or subtly, felt like you were on autopilot or not fully aware of what you were doing — even if it wasn’t as noticeable or extreme as the fair incident?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess the fair thing might count, yeah. I don’t like saying it that way, because it makes me sound like I was trying to cause trouble, and I wasn’t.\n\nI was giving that talk, and then I climbed up on the tractor and started working the controls, kind of jerking them around to show everybody this “new pattern” I thought I needed to demonstrate. People were yelling for me to stop, and I heard them, I think, but I just… I couldn’t seem to make myself stop right then.\n\nSo maybe that was like being on autopilot. Not like I blacked out, exactly, but I wasn’t fully in charge of myself the way I should’ve been. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: While you were moving the tractor controls at the fair, did you feel any changes in your perception, like things seemed unreal, distant, or distorted?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I can put my finger on.\n\nIt wasn’t like the place looked warped or far away, or like I was dreaming, if that’s what you mean. I knew I was at the fair and people were there. I was just very caught up in what I thought I was doing, I guess. I don’t want to make it sound stranger than it was.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Mostly just that fair incident, I think. I don’t want to overstate it like it happens all the time in normal chores or anything.

But yes… when I was up there giving the talk, I climbed onto the tractor and started moving the controls, kind of jerking them around to show everyone this “new pattern” I thought I had to demonstrate. People were yelling at me to stop, and I could hear them, but I didn’t really seem to be deciding it in a normal way. I just kept going.

So I guess that was me not being fully aware of why, or not being fully in control for a bit. I’m not trying to make excuses — that’s just the closest thing I can think of.

Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: While you were moving the tractor controls at the fair, did you feel like you were in a daze or not fully aware of what was happening around you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I was in a daze exactly… I knew I was at the fair, and I knew people were there watching. I just got so caught up in showing that “new pattern” on the tractor that I kept jerking the controls, even after folks started yelling at me to stop.\n\nI’m not proud of that. It wasn’t like I meant to ignore them, but in the moment I couldn’t seem to make myself stop right away. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: While you were moving the tractor controls, did you feel like you were acting automatically, as if you were on autopilot and not fully in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yeah… not like I was unconscious or anything. I knew I was up there, and I knew people were watching.\n\nBut once I started jerking the controls to show that “new pattern,” it sort of got away from me. People were yelling for me to stop, and I should’ve stopped right then, I know that… but I couldn’t seem to make myself quit right away. I don’t want to make it sound like I meant to cause trouble. It just felt like I wasn’t fully steering myself in that moment, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when you felt like you were acting automatically or not fully aware of what you were doing, outside of that day at the fair?\n\nPatient: I can’t think of a lot of other times like that, no… not the same way.\n\nThe fair was the main one they brought me in for. I climbed up on that tractor during my talk, and I got to jerking the controls to show everybody that “new pattern” I was trying to explain. People were yelling at me to stop, and I… I just didn’t stop right away, even though I should have.\n\nOutside of that, I don’t know that I’d say I’ve had another episode quite like it. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but that day, yeah, it did feel like I wasn’t fully in charge of myself for a bit. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: While you were moving the tractor controls at the fair, did you feel confused about who you were or feel like you were watching yourself from the outside?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it was like that. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: While you were moving the tractor controls at the fair, did you feel confused about who you were or feel like you were watching yourself from the outside?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it was like that. I knew I was me, and I don’t remember feeling like I was floating outside myself or watching it happen from somewhere else.\n\nI was embarrassed after, and I know I should’ve stopped sooner, but at the time it wasn’t confusion about who I was. More like I got wrapped up in what I was doing. Is that the distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself doing something automatically or without full awareness, even briefly, outside of the fair incident — like you were going through the motions without being fully in control?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, everybody has moments where you’re doing chores and your mind wanders a little, but nothing like that fair thing, where I felt like I couldn’t stop right away.\n\nI haven’t noticed myself blanking out or doing things without knowing it, if that’s what you mean. At least, not that I can point to. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any moments, even brief ones, where you felt like you were acting automatically or not fully aware of what you were doing, even outside of the fair incident?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know I keep circling back to the same thing… but the fair is really the only one that stands out.\n\nWhen they brought me in, it was because I’d climbed up onto that tractor during my talk, and I was jerking the controls around trying to show the crowd this “new pattern” I had in my head. People were yelling for me to stop, and I heard them, but I just… didn’t stop right away. It felt like I couldn’t quite make myself quit in that moment.\n\nOutside of that, I can’t honestly think of another time like that. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m worse than I am, but that one did happen. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a moment where you suddenly realized you were doing something but couldn’t remember why you started doing it in the first place?\n\nPatient: Not that I can really remember, no.
